<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Every single log file contains "python: command not found"! The training script used `python` instead of `python3`, and the server doesn't have `python` in the PATH. This is why:

1. The training never ran
2. The inference never ran
3. The evaluation never ran
4. But the pipeline script continued because `| tee` swallowed the error code (or `set -eo pipefail` wasn't actually applied properly)
5. The COMPLETE marker was written anyway (suggesting the pipeline script just ran through all stages without checking exit codes)

This is a critical failure — the entire P1 EE FT experiment produced no results but the pipeline marked itself as complete. 

Now I need to verify that all the logs show this same error and check when the COMPLETE marker was actually written.
```

{
  "text": "[severity: critical] Report #28 — P1 EE FT 实验完全失败：`python: command not found`\n\n## 发现\n\nP1 EE FT pipeline 所有 7 个阶段（training, unconstrained infer, constrained infer, eval ×2, violation ×2）的日志内容完全相同：\n\n```\nbash: line 1: python: command not found\n```\n\nPipeline 脚本未检查命令退出码，直接写入了 `p1_ee_ft_COMPLETE` 标记（内容 \"=== ALL DONE ===\"），导致 cron 误报实验完成。\n\n## 证据\n\n- 7 个 log 文件全部为同一行 \"python: command not found\"\n- checkpoints/ 目录为空（无 LoRA 训练产出）\n- 无任何 FT 推理输出文件\n- COMPLETE 标记存在但内容为空洞的 \"=== ALL DONE ===\"\n- westd-21461 GPU 0: 0% util, 0 MiB\n\n## 根因\n\nPipeline 脚本使用 `python` 而非 `python3`。westd-21461 上 `python` 不在 PATH 中。加上 `| tee` 吞错误码的已知问题（Report #26 已提及第一次崩溃），pipeline 在每个阶段都静默失败后写入完成标记。\n\n## 修复\n\n1. 脚本中所有 `python` 替换为 `python3`\n2. Pipeline 脚本加 `set -eo pipefail`（确保真正生效，不被 `| tee` 绕过）\n3. 每个阶段完成后检查输出文件存在性，不存在则 abort\n4. 删除虚假 COMPLETE 标记\n5. 重跑全部 pipeline\n\n这是第三次 P1 启动失败。建议 Agent 在 tmux 内手动逐步验证每个阶段，而非依赖一键 pipeline。",

}

```text
The script looks solid:

1. ✅ `set -eo pipefail` — errors will abort the pipeline
2. ✅ `source /root/miniconda3/bin/activate` — correct Python path
3. ✅ `WANDB_DISABLED=true` — no wandb dependency
4. ✅ Environment check at the start (python version, torch, CUDA)
5. ✅ Checkpoint existence checks after training (`ls -la checkpoints/... || exit 1`)
6. ✅ Uses `python3` throughout (not `python`)


7. ✅ `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0`

But there's a critical gap: the script is missing `TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1` and `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1`, which are essential to prevent the model from attempting to download files from HuggingFace Hub during `adjust_generation_fn()`. This was the exact cause of the previous r2 crash, and without these flags, it'll fail the same way again.
```

I can see from the first doc:
- The 70B model outputs relation types like "location", "part of", "country of origin", "available on", "member of", "inception" — these are **natural language relation names**, not DocRED's P-codes (P17, P159, P127 etc.)
- This means the model will have HIGH RV (Relation Vocabulary) violations since DocRED uses Wikidata property IDs
- Entity types look reasonable: ORG, LOC, TIME, NUM — but "NUM" is not a DocRED entity type (DocRED uses NUM as well? Let me think... 

Actually, the natural language relation types make sense for unconstrained zero-shot prompting — the 8B models showed similar high RV rates (Qwen at 29.66%, Llama at 16.03%), so the constrained version should address this. The real question is whether the 70B baseline violation rate is actually lower than the 8B models, which would support the scaling hypothesis. I'll wait for the Agent to finish analyzing before checking the metrics.

The 998 documents parsed correctly with valid JSON output, and I'm noticing the 70B model (Llama-3.1-70B AWQ-INT4) is generating natural language relation names just like the 8B Llama did, so that's consistent behavior across model sizes. Let me check if the constrained run has finished while waiting for the metrics to populate.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Interesting! The constrained output for doc 1 (Skai TV) has the SAME relation types as unconstrained — "location", "part of", "country of origin" etc. These are **NOT** DocRED P-code relation types!

This is a major red flag. The grammar layer should have forced relation types to be from the DocRED vocabulary (P17, P159, P127, etc.). If the constrained output still has natural language relation names, it means either:

1. The grammar constraint wasn't applied properly
2. The 70B model's tokenization is different (AWQ-INT4 quantization)


3. The constraint file doesn't match the 70B model's vocabulary

The mode field does show "full_s1", so the constrained setting was active, yet the relation types are still in natural language. This suggests the grammar either failed silently, was misconfigured for this model, or is actually permitting natural language relation names instead of enforcing the P-code vocabulary.

Looking more closely at the outputs, I'm noticing something critical: the unconstrained and constrained results are nearly identical—same entities, same relations, same natural language types, with only the mode field and inference time differing. If the constraints were actually working, I'd expect the relation types to be P-codes instead of natural language descriptions. The fact that they're the same across both modes suggests the constraint layer either got bypassed entirely or had no real effect on the output.

I need to examine more documents to see if this pattern holds or if the first one just happened to produce valid outputs by chance. Since "location" and "part of" definitely aren't in DocRED's vocabulary, I should specifically look for P-code relation types in the other samples.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I can see the full picture. The constrained output contains a MIX of:
1. **P-codes** (valid DocRED relation types): P17, P131, P150, P159, P127, P27, P264, P175, P577, P749, P571, P355, P30, P108, P361, P527, P206, P495, P569, P69, P102, P19
2. **Natural language relation names** (INVALID for DocRED): "location", "part of", "country of origin", "located in the administrative territorial entity", "operator", "member of", "inception", "creator", "parent organization", "author", "producer", "developer", "instance of", "has part", "followed by", "present in work", "point in time", "start time", "end time", "located in or next to body of water", "member of political party", "place of birth", "date of birth", "educated at", "work location", "spouse

", "cast member", "publication date", "owned by", "country of citizenship"

This is a significant finding—the constrained mode should force all relation types to be P-codes, yet many documents still contain natural language types. Looking at the raw output versus parsed output for doc 1, both have natural language types despite the mode being "full_s1", which suggests either the grammar is falling back when semantic constraints are empty, or the grammar itself isn't properly enforcing the P-code vocabulary for the 70B model.

The most likely culprit is that the XGrammar constraint is tokenizer-specific—it was compiled for Qwen3-8B's tokenizer, but the 70B model uses a completely different tokenizer from Llama-3.1-70B-Instruct-AWQ-INT4, so the constraints might be ineffective. But wait, the 70B SciERC experiments already showed perfect constraint adherence with zero violations, which means the grammar must be recompiled per-model. So the issue is more nuanced than a simple tokenizer mismatch.

Looking more carefully at the grep output, I'm seeing a mix of entity types (ORG, LOC, PER, TIME, NUM, MISC), gold relation types in P-code format (P17, P131, P159), and predicted relation types that could be either natural language or P-codes. The problem might be that with DocRED's 96 relation types versus SciERC's 7, the grammar allows more flexibility, or the natural language names appearing in predictions suggest the constraints failed for certain documents.

The real question is whether the constrained output actually produces more P-codes than unconstrained output. Since the first document's constrained predictions matched the unconstrained ones exactly with natural language types, it looks like the constraints had no effect there. But other documents show P-codes in the constrained output, which could mean the constraints are working selectively. I need to count how many P-codes appear in constrained versus unconstrained predictions to see if there's a meaningful difference, though the grep output is too large to do manually.

Actually, this pattern aligns with the grammar-first fallback behavior from the paper. When constraints eliminate all valid tokens, the grammar-only mode takes over, but if the grammar allows arbitrary strings for relation types instead of just enumerating valid P-codes, then natural language would slip through even under constraints. This suggests either a bug in how the grammar was compiled for the 70B tokenizer or an issue with the AWQ quantization affecting token generation. Either way, this discrepancy needs to be investigated and flagged in the evaluation.

I'm waiting for the Agent's analysis to see if they detect RV violations under full_s1 for the 70B DocRED run — if RV exceeds 0%, that would be unprecedented since all previous constrained runs achieved near-zero violations. My suspicion is that the AWQ quantization model might have tokenizer issues that expose problems with the larger 96-relation vocabulary in DocRED, whereas SciERC's smaller 7-relation set worked fine. I'll let the evaluation finish and then verify their results.
```

_source line 5713 · 2026-05-18T21:44:05.490Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Interesting! Both unconstrained and constrained have exactly 985 lines matching `"type": "P[0-9]`. This grep counts the number of LINES (not occurrences per line) that contain at least one P-code relation type.

Wait — these are gold relation types in the input data, not the model's predicted types! The gold_relations field always uses P-codes. So 985 matches just means 985 documents (out of 998) have at least one gold relation with a P-code type. This doesn't tell me about the model's predicted output.

I need to be more specific and only grep the predicted/parsed relation types, not the gold ones. 

The structure is clear: gold_relations always contains P-codes, while parsed_output.relations contains the model's actual predictions in natural language. Since both are on the same JSON line, grep can't separate them, which explains why the P-code count is identical in both files. The real question is whether the constrained run's parsed output actually contains P-codes or natural language types.
```

</details>

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am the independent Research Reviewer (Loop #17, continued session) for project `schema_semantic_constrained_ie`, targeting EMNLP 2026 (ARR May 25). My role is to independently verify experiment conclusions, check GPU resource compliance (L1 red line #4), audit Worker outputs, review paper claims against registry data, and report findings to Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. This session focused on:
   - Verifying P8c FT SciERC latency completion and paper integration
   - Tracking Report #22 narrative consistency fixes
   - Discovering and verifying fix for P1 EE FT data leakage (SFT dev = test set)
   - Tracking five failed P1 EE FT launch attempts (wandb, python path, network offline issues)
   - Conducting systemic environment risk review of running experiments
   - Initial review of 70B DocRED completion data (anomalous constrained output)
   - Responding to system-triggered ROADMAP review requests

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **OntoCoDec**: Two-layer constrained decoding (Grammar via XGrammar + Semantic via TypeConstraintProcessor)
   - **FT-first narrative reframe**: Paper shifted from zero-shot-first to fine-tuned-first framing
   - **Latency overhead measurements**: P8 ZS SciERC +13.1%, P8b ZS DocRED +2.43%, P8c FT SciERC +10.00% (all Blackwell GPU)
   - **Data leakage in SFT splits**: SFT dev set contained 19/20 test doc_ids — caught and fixed
   - **Environment issues on westd-21461**: No `python` (only miniconda `python3`), no network access (Errno 101), requires `TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1` + `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` + `WANDB_DISABLED=true`
   - **TRL SFTTrainer `adjust_generation_fn()`**: Attempts HF Hub download during training init, crashes on offline servers
   - **70B AWQ-INT4 constrained decoding anomaly**: Mixed P-codes and natural language relation types in constrained output
   - **Constraint tightness (κ)**: SciERC κ=0.333, DocRED κ=0.842; drives residual DR
   - **FT composability numbers**: DR≤0.20%, R-F1±0.6pp, CF≤1.18%

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/schema_outputs/p8c_latency_report.json`** (westd-21461)
     - P8c FT SciERC latency results, independently verified
     - unconstrained: n=551, mean=2.612s, median=2.355s; constrained: n=551, mean=2.873s, median=2.624s; overhead: mean=+10.0%, median=+11.4%
   
   - **`artifacts/_project/paper/section5_runtime.tex`** (L5-6)
     - Updated with P8c data: "Under LoRA fine-tuning, overhead is $+$10.0\% on SciERC, with absolute latency 37\% lower than zero-shot due to shorter, more structured outputs."

7. Pending Tasks:
   - **Verify 70B DocRED evaluation metrics** when Agent finishes ANALYZING — cross-check RV, DR, ET, CF rates against 8B baselines. Investigate anomalous constrained output (natural language relation types in full_s1 mode).
   - **Monitor P1 EE FT composability r3** launch and execution (script patched, ALLOCATED)
   - **Monitor w9 DocRED sampling r2** completion (~89% done)
   - **Track p8b_docred_latency registry fix** (Report #21, conclusion still shows "failed" but experiment succeeded with +2.43%)

8. Current Work:
   Immediately before this summary, I was reviewing the 70B DocRED completion data. Both experiments (unconstrained and constrained) completed with 998/998 documents each. Both are in ANALYZING status in the registry (no metrics yet).

   I discovered a significant anomaly in the constrained output: the first document ("Skai TV") has **identical** output to unconstrained — all relation types are natural language ("location", "part of", "country of origin") instead of DocRED P-codes (P17, P159, P127). The last document ("Eclipse") also shows all natural language types under full_s1 mode. However, grepping across the full file revealed some documents DO have P-codes (P17, P131, P150, P264, etc.) in the constrained output.

   This mix of P-codes and natural language relation types in constrained mode is anomalous — the grammar layer should force ALL relation types to be from the DocRED vocabulary (P-codes only). This contrasts with 70B SciERC which achieved DR=0%, RV=0% under constraints.

   For reference, 8B model baselines on DocRED:
   - Qwen unconstrained: DR=18.46%, RV=29.66%, ET=2.48%
   - Qwen constrained: DR=10.21%, RV=0.03%, ET=0%
   - Llama unconstrained: DR=26.07%, RV=16.03%
   - Llama constrained: DR=2.55%, RV=0%, ET=0%

   **GPU Status**:
   - westd-16980 GPU 0: 0% (70B unconstrained done)
   - westd-16980 GPU 1: 0% (70B constrained done) or finalizing
   - westd-21461 GPU 0: 0% (P1 EE FT r3 awaiting launch)
   - westd-21461 GPU 1: ~85% (w9 DocRED sampling r2, ~89%)

   **Registry Status**:
   - p1_ee_ft_composability_r3: ALLOCATED
   - 70b_docred_unconstrained: ANALYZING
   - 70b_docred_constrained: ANALYZING

   **Reports This Session**: #23-#31 (9 reports). **Total Across All Loops**: 31.

9. Optional Next Step:
   Wait for Agent to complete ANALYZING on both 70B DocRED experiments and populate registry metrics. Then independently verify the metrics, paying special attention to:
   1. Whether RV > 0% in constrained mode (would be anomalous — all previous constrained runs achieved RV ≈ 0%)
   2. Whether the natural language relation types in constrained output indicate grammar enforcement failure for the 70B AWQ-INT4 tokenizer on DocRED
   3. Compare 70B baseline violations against 8B models to assess the C6 model scaling claim
   4. Send audit report to Director with findings

   Also monitor P1 EE FT r3 launch (script is patched and ready, experiment ALLOCATED) and w9 DocRED r2 completion.

      "- claude: Catch-all for any task that doesn't fit a more specific agent. FleetView's default when no agent name is typed. (Tools: *)",
      "- Explore: Fast read-only search agent for locating code. Use it to find files by pattern (eg. \"src/components/**/*.tsx\"), grep for symbols or keywords (eg. \"API endpoints\"), or answer \"where is X defined / which files reference Y.\" Do NOT use it for code review, design-doc auditing, cross-file consistency checks, or open-ended analysis — it reads excerpts rather than whole files and will miss content past its read window. When calling, specify search breadth: \"quick\" for a single targeted lookup, \"medium\" for moderate exploration, or \"very thorough\" to search across multiple locations and naming conventions. (Tools: All tools except Agent, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)",
      "- general-purpose: General-purpose agent for researching complex questions, searching for code, and executing multi-step tasks. When you are searching for a keyword or file and are not confident that you will find the right match in the first few tries use this agent to perform the search for you. (Tools: *)",
      "- Plan: Software architect agent for designing implementation plans. Use this when you need to plan the implementation strategy for a task. Returns step-by-step plans, identifies critical files, and considers architectural trade-offs. (Tools: All tools except Agent, ExitPlanMode, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit)",
